is even. We remind the reader that the radiative fields of Eqs.(
277
) and (
212
) as well as the power loss formula Eq.(
307
) are still valid with an appropriate definition of double factorials. The main difference now is that the power loss above can be reversed by time-reversing the charges/currents.
Such a reversible change in energy can then be absorbed into a redefinition of energy.
Physically, when
d
is even, the radiation lingers on around the source, and its back-reaction serves to
renormalise
the source properties without any dissipative effects. We will see below that Eq.(
307
) should really be interpreted as a beta function in classical EM.

Before proceeding, we would like to recast Eq.(
307
) in terms of cartesian multipole tensors. Using Eq.(
295
), we have

\begin{split}&\sum_{\vec{m}}|\mathcal{J}^{E}(\omega,\ell,\vec{m})|^{2}=\frac{1}{[2\nu(\ell+d-2)]^{2}}\\
&\times\int_{0}^{\infty}dr_{1}\int_{\hat{r}_{1}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\ r_{1}^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}-1}{}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r_{1}^{2}}{4}\right]\int_{0}^{\infty}dr_{2}\int_{\hat{r}_{2}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\ r_{2}^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}-1}{}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r_{2}^{2}}{4}\right]\\
&\qquad\times\Bigl\{i\omega r_{1}^{2}J^{r}(\vec{r}_{1},\omega)-\partial_{r_{1}}[r_{1}^{2}J^{t}(\vec{r}_{1},\omega)]\Bigr\}^{\ast}\Bigl\{i\omega r_{2}^{2}J^{r}(\vec{r}_{2},\omega)-\partial_{r_{2}}[r_{2}^{2}J^{t}(\vec{r}_{2},\omega)]\Bigr\}\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\sum_{\vec{m}}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell\vec{m}}(\hat{r}_{1})\ \mathscr{Y}^{\ast}_{\ell\vec{m}}(\hat{r}_{2})\ \ .\end{split}

(308)

The sum appearing in the last line can be performed by invoking the SSH addition theorem (Eq.(
56
)), and the answer factorised via symmetry/idempotence of the SSH projector:

\begin{split}\sum_{\vec{m}}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell\vec{m}}(\hat{r}_{1})\ \mathscr{Y}^{\ast}_{\ell\vec{m}}(\hat{r}_{2})&=\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}\ \Pi^{S}(\hat{r}_{1}|\hat{r}_{2})_{d,\ell}\\
&=\frac{1}{\ell!\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}\ (\Pi^{S})^{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}_{<j_{1}\ldots j_{\ell}>}\hat{r}_{1}^{j_{1}}\ldots\hat{r}_{1}^{j_{\ell}}\ (\Pi^{S})^{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}_{<k_{1}\ldots k_{\ell}>}\hat{r}_{2}^{k_{1}}\ldots\hat{r}_{2}^{k_{\ell}}\ .\end{split}

(309)

In the next step, we use the definition of STF electric moment in Eq.(
298
) to write

\begin{split}&\sum_{\vec{m}}|\mathcal{J}^{E}(\omega,\ell,\vec{m})|^{2}=\frac{1}{(2\nu)^{2}\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}\frac{1}{\ell!}\ \left[{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]^{\ast}\ \left[{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]\ .\end{split}

(310)

A similar derivation can be given for the magnetic moment. From Eq.(
209
), we have

\begin{split}\sum_{\alpha\vec{m}}|\mathcal{J}^{B}(\omega,\alpha,\ell,\vec{m})|^{2}&=\frac{1}{(2\nu)^{2}}\int_{\vec{r}_{1}}\ r_{1}^{\nu-\frac{d}{2}+2}{}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r_{1}^{2}}{4}\right]\int_{\vec{r}_{2}}\ r_{2}^{\nu-\frac{d}{2}+2}{}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r_{2}^{2}}{4}\right]\\
&\qquad\times\Bigl\{J^{I}(\vec{r}_{1},\omega)\Bigr\}^{\ast}\Bigl\{J^{J}(\vec{r}_{1},\omega)\Bigr\}\sum_{\alpha\vec{m}}\mathbb{V}_{I}^{\alpha\ell\vec{m}}(\hat{r}_{1})\ \mathbb{V}_{J}^{\alpha\ell\vec{m}\ast}(\hat{r}_{2})\ \ .\end{split}

(311)

We can relate the spherical components of the currents to
cartesian ones by writing
J^{I}(\vec{r},\omega)=J^{i}(\vec{r},\omega)\frac{\partial\vartheta^{I}}{\partial x^{i}}
.
Applying the VSH addition theorem as well as the symmetry/idempotence of the VSH projector, we get

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\vartheta_{1}^{I}}{\partial x_{1}^{i}}\frac{\partial\vartheta_{2}^{J}}{\partial x_{2}^{j}}&\sum_{\alpha\vec{m}}\mathbb{V}_{I}^{\alpha\ell\vec{m}}(\hat{r}_{1})\ \mathbb{V}_{J}^{\alpha\ell\vec{m}\ast}(\hat{r}_{2})\\
&=\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|r_{1}r_{2}}\ \Pi^{V}_{ij}(\hat{r}_{1}|\hat{r}_{2})\\
&=\frac{1}{\ell!\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|r_{1}r_{2}}\ (\Pi^{V})^{k<k_{1}\ldots k_{\ell}>}_{i<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\hat{r}_{1}^{i_{1}}\ldots\hat{r}_{1}^{i_{\ell}}\ (\Pi^{V})_{j<j_{1}\ldots j_{\ell}>}^{k<k_{1}\ldots k_{\ell}>}\hat{r}_{2}^{j_{1}}\ldots\hat{r}_{2}^{j_{\ell}}\ .\end{split}

(312)

We can then use the definition of
{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}
given in Eq.(
214
) to write

\begin{split}&\sum_{\alpha\vec{m}}|\mathcal{J}^{B}(\omega,\alpha,\ell,\vec{m})|^{2}=\frac{1}{(2\nu)^{2}\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}\frac{1}{\ell!}\ \left[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{k<k_{1}\ldots k_{\ell}>}\right]^{\ast}\ \left[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{k<k_{1}\ldots k_{\ell}>}\right]\ .\end{split}

(313)

Putting these results together and using the explicit formula for
\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}
, we can rewrite the power loss formula in Eq.(
307
) entirely in terms of STF moments:

\begin{split}\mathcal{P}(\omega)&=\frac{1}{(d-2)!!|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{\ell+d-2}{\ell}\frac{\omega^{2\ell+d-1}}{(2\ell+d-2)!!}\ \frac{1}{\ell!}\ \left[{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]^{\ast}\ \left[{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{(d-2)!!|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{\omega^{2\ell+d-1}}{(2\ell+d-2)!!}\ \frac{1}{\ell!}\ \left[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{i<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]^{\ast}\ \left[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{i<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]\ .\end{split}

(314)

For odd
d
, the case where there is a dissipative power loss, the above expression can be rewritten in the following suggestive form:

\begin{split}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\mathcal{P}(\omega)&=\int_{0}^{\infty}\omega\ \frac{\omega^{d-1}d\omega}{(2\pi)^{d}2\omega}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{\ell+d-2}{\ell}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}{\ell!}\ \left[\omega^{\ell}\ {}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]^{\ast}\ \left[\omega^{\ell}\ {}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{\infty}\omega\ \frac{\omega^{d-1}d\omega}{(2\pi)^{d}2\omega}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}{\ell!}\ \left[\omega^{\ell}\ {}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{i<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]^{\ast}\ \left[\omega^{\ell}\ {}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{i<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]\ .\end{split}

(315)

We recognise in front the Lorentz-invariant phase-space integral for a photon of energy
\omega
, as well as another factor of
\omega
, indicating that we are computing its energy. Since this is power loss, the remaining factor should be interpreted as the production rate of photon by a given multipole moment. This can be made even more explicit if we recognise each term in the sum as the inner product on the sphere for SSHs and VSHs: See
Eq.(
57
) and Eq.(
106
). We then have the following integral representations:

\begin{split}\int_{\hat{k}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}&\left[\frac{1}{\ell!}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\hat{k}^{i_{1}}\ldots\hat{k}^{i_{\ell}}\right]^{\ast}\left[\frac{1}{\ell!}\ {}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<j_{1}\ldots j_{\ell}>}\hat{k}^{j_{1}}\ldots\hat{k}^{j_{\ell}}\right]\\
&=\frac{\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}{\ell!}\ \left[{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]^{\ast}\ \left[{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]\ ,\\
\int_{\hat{k}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}&\left[\frac{1}{\ell!}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<pi_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1}>}\hat{k}^{i_{1}}\ldots\hat{k}^{i_{\ell-1}}\right]^{\ast}\left[\frac{1}{\ell!}\ {}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<pj_{1}\ldots j_{\ell-1}>}\hat{k}^{j_{1}}\ldots\hat{k}^{j_{\ell-1}}\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{\ell^{2}}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell-1}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}{(\ell-1)!}\ \left[{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]^{\ast}\ \left[{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]\ ,\\
\int_{\hat{k}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}&\left[\frac{1}{\ell!}{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{p<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\hat{k}^{i_{1}}\ldots\hat{k}^{i_{\ell}}\right]^{\ast}\left[\frac{1}{\ell!}\ {}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{p<j_{1}\ldots j_{\ell}>}\hat{k}^{j_{1}}\ldots\hat{k}^{j_{\ell}}\right]\\
&=\frac{\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}{\ell!}\ \left[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{i<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]^{\ast}\ \left[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{i<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]\ .\end{split}

(316)

These, along with the identity

\frac{1}{\ell^{2}}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell-1}}{(\ell-1)!}-\frac{\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}}{\ell!}=\frac{\ell+d-2}{\ell}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}}{\ell!}

(317)

allows us to write the power loss as the Lorentz-invariant phase-space integral for the with momentum
\vec{k}
and energy
\omega_{k}\equiv|\vec{k}|
, i.e.,

\begin{split}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\mathcal{P}(\omega)&=\int\frac{d^{d}k}{(2\pi)^{d}2\omega_{k}}\times\omega_{k}(\delta^{pq}-\hat{k}^{p}\hat{k}^{q})\\
&\qquad\times\Biggl\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left[\frac{\omega_{k}}{\ell!}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega_{k})_{<pi_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1}>}k^{i_{1}}\ldots k^{i_{\ell-1}}\right]^{\ast}\left[\frac{\omega_{k}}{\ell!}\ {}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega_{k})_{<qj_{1}\ldots j_{\ell-1}>}k^{j_{1}}\ldots k^{j_{\ell-1}}\right]\\
&\qquad\qquad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left[\frac{1}{\ell!}{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega_{k})_{p<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}k^{i_{1}}\ldots k^{i_{\ell}}\right]^{\ast}\left[\frac{1}{\ell!}\ {}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega_{k})_{q<j_{1}\ldots j_{\ell}>}k^{j_{1}}\ldots k^{j_{\ell}}\right]\Biggr\}\ .\end{split}

(318)

Here we have used the fact that
{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}_{p<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\hat{k}^{p}k^{i_{1}}\ldots k^{i_{\ell}}=0
due to the transversality. The factor
(\delta^{pq}-\hat{k}^{p}\hat{k}^{q})
is the polarisation sum, summing over all transverse polarisations of the photon. The power loss written above corresponds to the following photon emission amplitudes by the multipoles:

\begin{split}&i(-i)^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\varepsilon^{p\ast}(\vec{k})}{\ell!}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega_{k})_{<pi_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1}>}\omega_{k}k^{i_{1}}\ldots k^{i_{\ell-1}}\ ,\qquad i(-i)^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\varepsilon^{p\ast}(\vec{k})}{\ell!}{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega_{k})_{p<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}k^{i_{1}}\ldots k^{i_{\ell}}\ .\end{split}

(319)

Here
\varepsilon^{p}(\vec{k})
is the polarisation for the photon with momentum
\vec{k}
and energy
\omega_{k}\equiv|\vec{k}|
, and we have fixed the overall phase by comparing against radiative fields in Eq.(
284
) and Eq.(
222
).

C.2
EM influence phase

We will now turn to the description of radiation reaction in flat spacetime. Our goal here is to get some sort of effective action that captures the effect of radiation on charge/current sources. Since radiation carries away energy in some cases (not always: see below), what we need is an action that can describe dissipation. This implies that the correct language here is that of
influence phase
ala Feynman-Vernon
[
31
]
, i.e., an action that doubles the system degrees of freedom to allow us to describe the evolution of density matrices, its decoherence and dissipation into the environment. As described by Feynman-Vernon, the influence phase is computed by doing a path integral over the doubled environment (here the EM fields) coupled to a doubled system(here the charges/currents), with a specific in-in boundary condition on the environment fields. As emphasised by us in
[
14
]
, such in-in boundary conditions are naturally implemented on the dS-SK geometry built by connecting two static patches at the future horizon. In the subsequent appendices, we will show that this works also for electromagnetism in dS. Coming back to the current topic of flat space EM, there is no such simple geometric construction: the in-in boundary conditions have to be imposed by hand.
29
29

29

Strictly speaking, flat spacetime EM path integral also has a variety of infrared subtleties. We will ignore them in what follows.
For
d=3
, the reader can find such an analysis in
[
95
,
96
,
97
]
. Given that we will be presenting a detailed derivation of the influence phase in the dS case, we will be content here with a brief sketch.

We remind the reader that the current density can be parametrised in terms of two functions such that it solves the conservation equations in the following manner:

\begin{split}J^{t}(\vec{r},\omega)&=\sum_{\ell\vec{m}}\Bigl\{-\frac{1}{r^{d-1}}\frac{\partial}{\partial r}[r^{d-1}J_{1}(r,\omega,\ell,\vec{m})]+\frac{\ell(\ell+d-2)}{r^{2}}J_{2}(r,\omega,\ell,\vec{m})\Bigr\}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell\vec{m}}(\hat{r})\ ,\\
J^{r}(\vec{r},\omega)&=-i\omega\sum_{\ell\vec{m}}J_{1}(r,\omega,\ell,\vec{m})\mathscr{Y}_{\ell\vec{m}}(\hat{r})\ ,\\
r^{2}\gamma_{{}_{IK}}J^{K}(\vec{r},\omega)&=-i\omega\sum_{\ell\vec{m}}J_{2}(r,\omega,\ell,\vec{m})\mathscr{D}_{I}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell\vec{m}}(\hat{r})+r^{2}\gamma_{{}_{IK}}\sum_{\alpha\ell\vec{m}}J_{V}(r,\omega,\alpha,\ell,\vec{m})\mathbb{V}^{K}_{\alpha\ell\vec{m}}\ .\end{split}

(320)

We will now consider two copies of these currents(
J_{L}
and
J_{R}
), which can be independently specified for all cases except in the
\ell=0
case.

\begin{split}&\int_{\omega}\int dr\int dr_{0}\\
&\Bigl\{\sum_{\ell\vec{m}}\ell(\ell+d-2)\left[J_{1}(r,\omega,\ell,\vec{m})-\partial_{r}J_{2}(r,\omega,\ell,\vec{m})\right]^{*}_{D}\mathbb{G}_{E}(r,r_{0},\omega,\ell)\left[J_{1}(r_{0},\omega,\ell,\vec{m})-\partial_{r_{0}}J_{2}(r_{0},\omega,\ell,\vec{m})\right]_{A}\Bigr.\\
&\qquad\Bigl.\qquad+\sum_{\alpha\ell\vec{m}}[J_{V}(r,\omega,\alpha,\ell,\vec{m})]^{*}_{D}\mathbb{G}_{B}(r,r_{0},\omega,\ell)J_{V}(r_{0},\omega,\alpha,\ell,\vec{m})_{A}\Bigr\}\ \end{split}

(321)

For odd values of
d
,
\cot(\nu\pi)
is zero, and the renormalised action is given by:

\begin{split}\sum_{\ell\vec{m}}\ell(\ell+d-2)&\int_{\omega}\frac{\pi i}{2\Gamma(\nu+1)^{2}}\left(\frac{\omega}{2}\right)^{2\nu}\\
&\times\int dr_{0}\ r_{0}^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}-1}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r_{0}^{2}}{4}\right]\left[\partial_{r_{0}}J_{2}(r_{0},\omega)-J_{1}(r_{0},\omega)\right]^{*}_{D}\\
&\quad\times\int dr\ r^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}-1}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]\left[\partial_{r}J_{2}(r,\omega)-J_{1}(r,\omega)\right]_{A}\end{split}

(322)

This action for even values of
d
gets multiplied by a factor of
(1+i\cot(\nu\pi))
and hence diverges. Further counterterms are needed for regularisation. These counterterms can be computed by expanding the action around
\nu=n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Consider:

(1+i\cot\pi\nu)\frac{2\pi i}{\Gamma(\nu)^{2}}\left(\frac{\omega}{2H}\right)^{2\nu}=\frac{1}{\Gamma(n)^{2}}\left(\frac{\omega}{2H}\right)^{2n}\left\{\frac{2}{\nu-n}-4\psi^{(0)}(n)+\ln\left(\frac{\omega}{2H}\right)^{4}+O(\nu-n)\right\}\ .

(323)

where
H
is the renormalisation scale. Following the same modified minimal subtraction scheme proposed in
[
14
]
, we will counterterm the first two terms in the RHS. Using this scheme, the RR action becomes:

\begin{split}\sum_{\ell\vec{m}}\ell(\ell+d-2)&\int_{\omega}\frac{1}{4\Gamma(\nu+1)^{2}}\left(\frac{\omega}{2}\right)^{2\nu}\ln{\left(\frac{\omega^{4}}{H^{4}}\right)}\\
&\times\int dr_{0}\ r_{0}^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}-1}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r_{0}^{2}}{4}\right]\left[\partial_{r_{0}}J_{2}(r_{0},\omega)-J_{1}(r_{0},\omega)\right]^{*}_{D}\\
&\quad\times\int dr\ r^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}-1}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]\left[\partial_{r}J_{2}(r,\omega)-J_{1}(r,\omega)\right]_{A}\end{split}

(324)

A similar argument gives us the corresponding action for the magnetic parity action. We have:

\begin{split}S^{\text{vector, bare}}_{RR}=\sum_{\ell\vec{m}}\int_{\omega}\int r^{d-1}dr\int r_{0}^{d-1}dr_{0}&J_{VD}^{*}(r_{0},w,\ell,\vec{m})J_{VA}(r_{0},w,\ell,\vec{m})\mathbb{G}_{B}(r,r_{0};\ell,\vec{m})\end{split}

(325)

For odd values of
d
, the dissipative part of the action is given by:

\begin{split}\sum_{\ell\vec{m}}&\int_{\omega}\frac{\pi i}{2\Gamma(\nu+1)^{2}}\left(\frac{\omega}{2}\right)^{2\nu}\\
&\times\int dr_{0}\ r_{0}^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}+1}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r_{0}^{2}}{4}\right]J_{VD}^{*}(r_{0},w,\ell,\vec{m})\\
&\quad\times\int dr\ r^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}+1}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]J_{VA}^{*}(r_{0},w,\ell,\vec{m})\end{split}

(326)

Counterterming away the extra divergence for the case of even
d
in the same way as for the electric sector, we obtain the action:

\begin{split}\sum_{\ell\vec{m}}\ell(\ell+d-2)&\int_{\omega}\frac{1}{4\Gamma(\nu+1)^{2}}\left(\frac{\omega}{2}\right)^{2\nu}\ln{\left(\frac{\omega^{4}}{H^{4}}\right)}\\
&\times\int dr_{0}\ r_{0}^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}+1}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r_{0}^{2}}{4}\right]J_{VD}^{*}(r_{0},w,\ell,\vec{m})\\
&\quad\times\int dr\ r^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}+1}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]J_{VA}^{*}(r_{0},w,\ell,\vec{m})\end{split}

(327)

Given this reduced boundary action, we can write down an SK action for the EM radiation reaction as:

S_{\text{RR}}=\sum_{\ell\vec{m}}\frac{\ell+d-2}{\ell}\int_{\omega}\frac{2\pi i}{\Gamma(\nu)^{2}}\left(\frac{\omega}{2}\right)^{2\nu}\left\{\mathcal{J}^{D*}_{E}\mathcal{J}^{A}_{E}+\frac{\ell}{\ell+d-2}\mathcal{J}^{D*}_{B}\mathcal{J}^{A}_{B}\right\}

(328)

where we define:

\begin{split}\mathcal{J}_{A}^{E}(\omega,\ell,\vec{m})&\equiv\frac{\ell}{2\nu}\int dr\ \Bigg[J_{2}^{A}\ \partial_{r}\left\{r^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}-1}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]\right\}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+J^{A}_{1}\ r^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}-1}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]\Bigg]\ ,\\
\mathcal{J}_{D}^{E}(\omega,\ell,\vec{m})&\equiv\frac{\ell}{2\nu}\int dr\ \Bigg[J_{2}^{D}\ \partial_{r}\left\{r^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}-1}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]\right\}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+J_{1}^{D}\ r^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}-1}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]\Bigg]\ ,\\
\mathcal{J}_{A}^{B}(\omega,\alpha,\ell,\vec{m})&\equiv\frac{1}{2\nu}\int dr\Bigg\{J_{V}^{A}(\alpha,\ell,\vec{m})r^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}+1}{}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]\Bigg\}\ ,\\
\mathcal{J}_{D}^{B}(\omega,\alpha,\ell,\vec{m})&\equiv\frac{1}{2\nu}\int dr\Bigg\{J_{V}^{D}(\alpha,\ell,\vec{m})r^{\nu+\frac{d}{2}+1}{}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]\Bigg\}\end{split}

(329)

Later, we will see that the influence phase obtained in de Sitter reduces to Eq.(
328
) in the flat space limit.

To compute the radiation reaction of a single particle, it is much more convenient to work with STF scalar harmonics. Corresponding to appendix A3 of
[
14
]
, one can rewrite the RR action in terms of STF moments
\mathcal{Q}^{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}(\omega)
as:

\begin{split}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}_{A}^{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}(\omega)&\equiv\frac{1}{(\ell+d-2)}(\Pi^{S})^{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}_{<j_{1}j_{2}\ldots j_{\ell}>}\int d^{d}x\ x^{j_{1}}x^{j_{2}}\ldots x^{j_{\ell}}\\
&\qquad\times\Bigg[\frac{1}{r^{\ell+d-3}}\partial_{r}\left\{r^{\ell+d-2}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{r^{2}\omega^{2}}{4}\right]\right\}J^{t}_{A}(\vec{r},\omega)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+i\omega r\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{r^{2}\omega^{2}}{4}\right]J^{r}_{A}(\vec{r},\omega)\Bigg]\ ,\end{split}

(330)

\begin{split}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}_{D}^{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}(\omega)&\equiv\frac{1}{(\ell+d-2)}(\Pi^{S})^{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}_{<j_{1}j_{2}\ldots j_{\ell}>}\int d^{d}x\ x^{j_{1}}x^{j_{2}}\ldots x^{j_{\ell}}\\
&\qquad\times\Bigg[\frac{1}{r^{\ell+d-3}}\partial_{r}\left\{r^{\ell+d-2}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{r^{2}\omega^{2}}{4}\right]\right\}J^{t}_{D}(\vec{r},\omega)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+i\omega r\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{r^{2}\omega^{2}}{4}\right]J^{r}_{D}(\vec{r},\omega)\Bigg]\ .\end{split}

(331)

In terms of these STF multipole moments, we can write the RR action as follows:

S^{E}_{\text{RR}}=\sum_{\ell}\frac{\ell+d-2}{\ell}\int\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\frac{\pi i}{2\Gamma(\nu+1)^{2}}\left(\frac{\omega}{2}\right)^{2\nu}\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}\frac{1}{\ell!}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}_{D}^{\ast<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}^{A}_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\ .

(332)

The magnetic multipole moment can be written in terms of the vector STF projector:

\begin{split}{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}_{A}^{i<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\equiv(\Pi^{V})^{i<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}_{j<j_{1}j_{2}\ldots j_{\ell}>}\int d^{d}x\ x^{j_{1}}x^{j_{2}}\ldots x^{j_{\ell}}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{r^{2}\omega^{2}}{4}\right]J^{j}_{A}\\
{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}_{D}^{i<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\equiv(\Pi^{V})^{i<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}_{j<j_{1}j_{2}\ldots j_{\ell}>}\int d^{d}x\ x^{j_{1}}x^{j_{2}}\ldots x^{j_{\ell}}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[1+\nu,-\frac{r^{2}\omega^{2}}{4}\right]J^{j}_{D}\end{split}

(333)

Using the vector addition theorem (
111
), we can show that the magnetic part of the influence phase can be written in terms of the STF multipole moments as:

S^{B}_{\text{RR}}=\sum_{\ell}\int\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\frac{\pi i}{2\Gamma(\nu+1)^{2}}\left(\frac{\omega}{2}\right)^{2\nu}\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}\frac{1}{\ell!}{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}_{D,STF}^{\ast i<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\ {}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{A,STF}_{i<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\ .

(334)

C.3
Multipole expansion in
d=3

In
d=3
, we can trade the magnetic field 2-form for an axial vector field
\bar{B}_{i}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{ijk}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{C}}$}}_{jk}
. It is more convenient to deal with vectors than tensors, and the electric-magnetic duality is easier to see. We will describe here how the multipole expansion and radiation reaction formulae can be recast to make EM duality manifest.

At the level of orthonormal spherical harmonics, in
d=3
, all vector spherical harmonics can be replaced with the toroidal operator acting on scalar spherical harmonics, viz.,

\begin{split}\mathbb{V}_{I}^{1\ell m}(\hat{r})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell(\ell+1)}}\varepsilon_{IJ}\mathscr{D}^{J}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\ ,\end{split}

(335)

where the spherical indices are raised using the unit sphere metric.
However, if we do this, we must contend with the irrational factors of
\sqrt{\ell(\ell+1)}
everywhere in the spherical harmonic expansions. We will instead use the following strategy, motivated primarily by EM duality. We use a rescaled basis of VSHs
30
30

30

In what follows, it is useful to remember that

\begin{split}d\vartheta^{I}\mathbb{U}_{I}^{\ell m}(\hat{r})=\frac{1}{\ell}\left(\frac{d\vartheta}{\sin\vartheta}\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\varphi}-\sin\vartheta\ d\varphi\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\vartheta}\right){Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})=\overrightarrow{dr}\cdot\left\{-\frac{\hat{e}_{r}}{\ell}\times\vec{\nabla}{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\right\}\ .\end{split}

(336)

The last expression gives a cartesian form for our rescaled VSH.

\begin{split}\mathbb{U}_{I}^{\ell m}(\hat{r})\equiv\sqrt{\frac{\ell+1}{\ell}}\mathbb{V}_{I}^{1\ell m}(\hat{r})=\frac{1}{\ell}\varepsilon_{{}_{IJ}}\mathscr{D}^{J}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\ .\end{split}

(337)

Similarly, we scale the components appearing in the vector spherical harmonic expansions. We will add a
\vee
symbol to all scaled components to avoid confusion. This rescaling factor should also be included in orthonormality, addition theorem, etc. A rough thumb rule is to replace all occurrences of
\mathbb{V}_{I}
s in our formulae with
\mathbb{U}_{I}
s, but replace
\mathbb{V}_{I}^{\ast}
with
\frac{\ell}{\ell+1}\mathbb{U}_{I}^{\ast}
(apart from adding
\vee
symbol to the components). We will see an example of this below.

Toroidal magnetostatics in
d=3

Let us first see how this works in the simpler setting of magnetostatics: the generalisation to the full dynamical situation is straightforward.

In terms of the rescaled VSH, the magnetostatic expansion in Eq.(
164
) becomes

\begin{split}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{V}}$}}_{r}&=0\ ,\quad\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{V}}$}}_{I}\equiv\sum_{\ell m}\overline{\Phi}^{\vee}_{{}_{B}}(r,\ell,m)\mathbb{U}_{I}^{\ell m}(\hat{r})\ ,\\
\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{C}}$}}_{rI}&\equiv\sum_{\ell m}\overline{H}^{\vee}_{v}(r,\alpha,\ell,\vec{m})\mathbb{U}_{I}^{\ell m}(\hat{r})=\varepsilon_{{}_{IJ}}\sum_{\ell m}\frac{1}{\ell}\overline{H}_{v}^{\vee}(r,\ell,m)\mathscr{D}^{J}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\ ,\\
\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{C}}$}}_{IJ}&\equiv\sum_{\ell\vec{m}\alpha}\overline{H}^{\vee}_{vv}(r,\ell,m)\mathscr{D}_{[I}\mathbb{U}_{J]}^{\ell m}(\hat{r})=\varepsilon_{{}_{IJ}}\sum_{\ell m}(\ell+1)\overline{H}_{vv}^{\vee}(r,\ell,m)\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\ ,\end{split}

(338)

where, in the last step, we have used the identity

\begin{split}\mathscr{D}_{[I}\mathbb{U}_{J]}^{\ell m}(\hat{r})=\varepsilon_{{}_{IJ}}\varepsilon^{MN}\mathscr{D}_{M}\mathbb{U}_{N}^{\ell m}=(\ell+1)\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\ \varepsilon_{{}_{IJ}}\ .\end{split}

(339)

The vector magnetic field is obtained by stripping off the
\varepsilon_{{}_{rIJ}}=r^{2}\varepsilon_{{}_{IJ}}
factors in
\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{C}}$}}_{ij}
and then lowering the indices.The spherical/radial components of the vector magnetic field are

\begin{split}\overline{B}_{I}&=r^{2}\gamma_{IJ}\times\frac{1}{r^{2}}\sum_{\ell m}\frac{1}{\ell}\overline{H}_{v}^{\vee}(r,\ell,m)\mathscr{D}^{J}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})=\sum_{\ell m}\frac{1}{\ell}\overline{H}_{v}^{\vee}(r,\ell,m)\mathscr{D}_{I}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\ ,\\
\overline{B}_{r}&=\sum_{\ell m}\frac{\ell+1}{r^{2}}\overline{H}_{vv}^{\vee}(r,\ell,m)\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\ .\end{split}

(340)

The expression for the Debye field in Eq.(
166
) becomes

\begin{split}\overline{\Phi}_{{}_{B}}^{\vee}(r,\ell,m)&\equiv\frac{\ell}{\ell+1}\int_{\vec{r}_{0}}\mathbb{G}_{B}(r,r_{0};\ell)\mathbb{U}_{J}^{\ell m\ast}(\hat{r}_{0})\bar{J}^{J}(\vec{r}_{0})=\sqrt{\frac{\ell}{\ell+1}}\overline{\Phi}_{{}_{B}}(r,\alpha=1,\ell,m)\ .\end{split}

(341)

In the first step, there is a rescaling pre-factor
\frac{\ell}{\ell+1}
which multiplies
\mathbb{U}_{J}^{\ell m\ast}
in accordance with the thumb rule mentioned above. Similar factors appear in the definition of the magnetic moment:

\begin{split}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{J}}$}}^{\vee B}(\ell,m)&\equiv\frac{1}{2\ell+1}\frac{\ell}{\ell+1}\int_{\vec{r}_{0}}r_{0}^{\ell+1}\mathbb{U}_{J}^{\ell m\ast}(\hat{r}_{0})\bar{J}^{J}(\vec{r}_{0})=\sqrt{\frac{\ell}{\ell+1}}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{J}}$}}^{B}(\alpha=1,\ell,m)\ .\end{split}

(342)

The magnetic Debye field and the magnetic field components outside the sources take the form

\begin{split}\overline{\Phi}_{{}_{B}}^{\vee,\text{Out}}(r,\ell,m)&=\frac{\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{J}}$}}^{\vee B}(\ell,m)}{r^{\ell}}\ ,\\
\frac{1}{\ell}\overline{H}_{v}^{\vee\text{Out}}(r,\ell,m)&=-\frac{\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{J}}$}}^{\vee B}(\ell,m)}{r^{\ell+1}}\ ,\quad\ \frac{\ell+1}{r^{2}}\overline{H}_{vv}^{\vee\text{Out}}(r,\ell,m)=(\ell+1)\frac{\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{J}}$}}^{\vee B}(\ell,m)}{r^{\ell+2}}\ .\end{split}

(343)

This should be compared against the electrostatic expressions written in terms of electric multipole moments:

\begin{split}\overline{\Phi}_{{}_{E}}^{\vee,\text{Out}}(r,\ell,\vec{m})&=\frac{\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{J}}$}}^{E}(\ell,\vec{m})}{r^{\ell}}\ ,\\
\overline{E}_{s}^{\text{Out}}(r,\ell,\vec{m})&=-\frac{\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{J}}$}}^{E}(\ell,\vec{m})}{r^{\ell+d-2}}\ ,\quad\ \overline{E}_{r}^{\text{Out}}(r,\ell,\vec{m})=(\ell+d-2)\frac{\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{J}}$}}^{E}(\ell,\vec{m})}{r^{\ell+d-1}}\ .\end{split}

(344)

We see that the forms of the outside electrostatic vs magnetostatic fields agree in
d=3
. In this normalisation, EM duality acts by mapping electric to magnetic fields and
\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{J}}$}}^{E}
to
\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{J}}$}}^{\vee B}
.
As to the cartesian moments, in analogy with electrostatic multipole tensors in Eq.(
242
), we define
31
31

31

We use the result that
\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell-1}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|=\frac{4\pi}{(2\ell-1)!!}
for
d=3
.

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\ell!}\ {}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}^{\vee}_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}x^{i_{1}}\ldots x^{i_{\ell}}&\equiv\frac{4\pi}{(2\ell-1)!!}\sum_{m}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{J}}$}}^{\vee B}(\ell,m)r^{\ell}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\\
&=\frac{1}{\ell+1}\int_{\vec{r}_{0}}[\vec{r}_{0}\times\vec{\bar{J}}(\vec{r}_{0})]\cdot\vec{\nabla}_{0}\Pi^{S}(\vec{r}|\vec{r}_{0})_{d=3,\ell}\ .\end{split}

(345)

In the second line, we have used the expression for
\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{J}}$}}^{\vee B}
in Eq.(
342
), the explicit form of
\mathbb{U}_{I}
, as well as the SSH addition theorem. The integral appearing here can be evaluated as

\begin{split}\int_{\vec{r}_{0}}[\vec{r}_{0}\times\vec{\bar{J}}(\vec{r}_{0})]\cdot\vec{\nabla}_{0}\Pi^{S}(\vec{\kappa}|\vec{r}_{0})&=\frac{\ell}{\ell!}\kappa^{i_{1}}\ldots\kappa^{i_{\ell}}(\Pi^{S})^{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}_{<j_{1}j_{2}\ldots j_{\ell}>}\int_{\vec{r}_{0}}[\vec{r}_{0}\times\vec{\bar{J}}(\vec{r}_{0})]^{j_{1}}x_{0}^{j_{2}}\ldots x_{0}^{j_{\ell}}\ ,\end{split}

(346)

thus yielding a direct cartesian definition

\begin{split}{}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}^{\vee}_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}&\equiv\frac{\ell}{\ell+1}\int_{\vec{r}_{0}}[\vec{r}_{0}\times\vec{\bar{J}}(\vec{r}_{0})]^{<i_{1}}x_{0}^{i_{2}}\ldots x_{0}^{i_{\ell}>}\ .\end{split}

(347)

By construction, the normalisation here is fixed to ensure that EM duality maps the STF tensors
{}^{E}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}
to
{}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}^{\vee}
. The outside vector potential/magnetic field can be written in terms of this STF magnetic moment as

\begin{split}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{V}}$}}_{k}^{\text{Out}}&=-\varepsilon_{kij}x^{i}\sum_{\ell}{}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}^{\vee}_{<ji_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1}>}\frac{x^{i_{1}}\ldots x^{i_{\ell-1}}}{4\pi r^{2\ell+1}}\frac{(2\ell-1)!!}{\ell!}\\
&=-\varepsilon_{kij}\sum_{\ell}\frac{(-)^{\ell}}{\ell!}\partial^{i}\partial^{i_{1}}\partial^{i_{2}}\ldots\partial^{i_{\ell-1}}\Bigl\{\frac{{}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}^{\vee}_{<ji_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1}>}}{4\pi r}\Bigr\}\ ,\\
\overline{B}_{j}^{\text{Out}}&=\sum_{\ell}{}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}^{\vee}_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}[(2\ell+1)x^{j}x^{i_{\ell}}-r^{2}\ell\delta^{ji_{\ell}}]\frac{x^{i_{1}}\ldots x^{i_{\ell-1}}}{4\pi r^{2\ell+3}}\frac{(2\ell-1)!!}{\ell!}\ .\end{split}

(348)

As expected from EM duality, the magnetic field here has the same form as the outside electrostatic field in Eq.(
244
). The description in terms of the magnetic moment
{}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}^{\vee}
can be related to ones in terms of
{}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}
as follows: first, we begin by rewriting the VSH projector as

\begin{split}\Pi^{V}_{ij}(\vec{\kappa}|\vec{r})|_{d=3}=\frac{[\vec{\kappa}\times\vec{\nabla}_{\kappa}]_{i}[\vec{r}\times\vec{\nabla}]_{j}}{\ell(\ell+1)}\Pi^{S}(\vec{\kappa}|\vec{r})|_{d=3}\ .\end{split}

(349)

This follows from the fact that, in
d=3
, all the VSHs can be obtained by applying a single toroidal operator
[\vec{r}\times\vec{\nabla}]
on SSHs. To get orthonormal VSHs, we need to divide by a factor
\sqrt{\ell(\ell+1)}
. This means that the VSH addition theorem in
d=3
can be obtained by sandwiching the SSH addition theorem between two toroidal operators
[
72
,
73
,
74
]
. The relation between the two addition theorems then yields the above relation between the projectors.

In the next step, we write

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\ell!}{}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}_{i<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\kappa^{i_{1}}\ldots\kappa^{i_{\ell}}&\equiv\int_{\vec{r}_{0}}\Pi^{V}_{ij}(\vec{\kappa}|\vec{r}_{0})\bar{J}^{j}(\vec{r}_{0})\\
&=-\frac{1}{\ell}[\vec{\kappa}\times\vec{\nabla}_{\kappa}]_{i}\frac{1}{\ell+1}\int_{\vec{r}_{0}}[\vec{r}_{0}\times\vec{\bar{J}}(\vec{r}_{0})]\cdot\vec{\nabla}_{0}\Pi^{S}(\vec{\kappa}|\vec{r}_{0})\ .\end{split}

(350)

In RHS, we recognise the integral that defines the
{}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}^{\vee}
. Stripping off the
\kappa^{i}
’s, we get the relation between the two kinds of moments as

\begin{split}{}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}_{i<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}&=\frac{1}{\ell}\sum_{p=1}^{\ell}{}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}^{\vee}_{<ji_{1}\ldots\underline{i_{p}}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\ \varepsilon_{iji_{p}}\ .\end{split}

(351)

Here, the underlining on the
i_{p}
index indicates that it should be dropped, i.e., the indices of
{}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}^{\vee}
are
ji_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{p-1}i_{p+1}\ldots i_{\ell}
. We can invert this relation by rewriting Eq.(
349
) as

\begin{split}[\vec{\kappa}\times\vec{\nabla}_{\kappa}]_{i}\Pi^{V}_{ij}(\vec{\kappa}|\vec{r})|_{d=3}=-[\vec{r}\times\vec{\nabla}]_{j}\Pi^{S}(\vec{\kappa}|\vec{r})|_{d=3}\ .\end{split}

(352)

This is equivalent to the tensorial relation

\begin{split}\sum_{p=1}^{\ell}\varepsilon_{ii_{p}n}(\Pi^{V})^{i<ni_{1}\ldots\underline{i_{p}}\ldots i_{\ell}>}_{j<j_{1}j_{2}\ldots j_{\ell}>}&=-\sum_{p=1}^{\ell}\varepsilon_{jj_{p}n}(\Pi^{S})^{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}_{<nj_{1}\ldots\underline{j_{p}}\ldots j_{\ell}>}\ .\end{split}

(353)

Multiplying by
x_{0}^{j_{1}}x_{0}^{j_{2}}\ldots x_{0}^{j_{\ell}}\bar{J}^{n}(\vec{r}_{0})
and integrating, we get

\begin{split}{}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}^{\vee}_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}&=-\frac{1}{\ell+1}\sum_{p=1}^{\ell}{}^{B}\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{Q}}$}}_{i<ji_{1}\ldots\underline{i_{p}}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\ \varepsilon_{iji_{p}}\ .\end{split}

(354)

The relations Eq.(
351
) and Eq.(
354
) show that same information is contained in the STF tensors
{}^{B}\overline{\mathcal{Q}}
and

{}^{B}\overline{\mathcal{Q}}^{\vee}
.

Multipole radiation in
d=3

We will now move to describing the full radiative fields in
d=3
outside the sources. The decomposition of EM fields in terms of spherical harmonics has the form

\begin{split}E_{I}(\vec{r},\omega)&=\sum_{\ell m}E_{s}(r,\omega,\ell,m)\mathscr{D}_{I}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})+\sum_{\ell m}E_{v}^{\vee}(r,\omega,\ell,m)\mathbb{U}_{I}^{\ell m}(\hat{r})\ ,\\
E_{r}(\vec{r},\omega)&=\sum_{\ell m}E_{r}(r,\omega,\ell,m)\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\ ,\\
B_{I}(\vec{r},\omega)&=\sum_{\ell m}\frac{1}{\ell}H_{v}^{\vee}(r,\omega,\ell,m)\mathscr{D}_{I}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})-\sum_{\ell m}\ell\ H_{s}(r,\omega,\ell,m)\mathbb{U}_{I}^{\ell m}(\hat{r})\ ,\\
B_{r}(\vec{r},\omega)&=\sum_{\ell m}\frac{\ell+1}{r^{2}}H_{vv}^{\vee}(r,\omega,\ell,m)\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\ .\end{split}

(355)

We can write this succinctly as

\begin{split}\vec{E}(\vec{r},\omega)&=\sum_{\ell m}\left[E_{r}(r,\omega,\ell,m)\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}\hat{e}_{r}+E_{s}(r,\omega,\ell,m)\ \vec{\nabla}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}+E_{v}^{\vee}(r,\omega,\ell,m)\ \vec{\mathbb{U}}_{\ell m}\right]\ ,\\
\vec{B}(\vec{r},\omega)&=\sum_{\ell m}\left[\frac{\ell+1}{r^{2}}H_{vv}^{\vee}(r,\omega,\ell,m)\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}\hat{e}_{r}+\frac{1}{\ell}H_{v}^{\vee}(r,\omega,\ell,m)\ \vec{\nabla}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}-\ell H_{s}(r,\omega,\ell,m)\ \vec{\mathbb{U}}_{\ell m}\right]\ ,\\
\end{split}

(356)

where we have introduced

\vec{\mathbb{U}}_{\ell m}\equiv-\frac{1}{\ell}\hat{e}_{r}\times\vec{\nabla}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}\ ,\quad\vec{\nabla}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}=\ell\ \hat{e}_{r}\times\vec{\mathbb{U}}_{\ell m}\ .

(357)

With this understanding, much of our description of
EM fields in this section continue to hold true. Explicit expressions for outside fields in terms of multipole moments can be written down using Eq.(
211
) and Eq.(
275
). We have

\begin{split}\vec{E}^{\text{Out}}(\vec{r},\omega)&=\sum_{\ell m}G_{{}_{E}}^{\text{Out}}(r,\omega,\ell)\ \mathcal{J}^{E}(\omega,\ell,m)\left[\frac{\ell+1}{r^{2}}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}\hat{e}_{r}-\frac{1}{r}\ \vec{\nabla}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}\right]\\
&+\sum_{\ell m}\frac{\omega^{2}}{\ell(2\ell-1)}G_{{}_{E}}^{\text{Out}}(r,\omega,\ell-1)\ \mathcal{J}^{E}(\omega,\ell,m)\vec{\nabla}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}\\
&+i\omega\ \sum_{\ell m}G_{{}_{B}}^{\text{Out}}(r,\omega,\ell)\ \mathcal{J}^{\vee B}(\omega,\ell,m)\ \vec{\mathbb{U}}_{\ell m}\ ,\\
\vec{B}^{\text{Out}}(\vec{r},\omega)&=\sum_{\ell m}G_{{}_{B}}^{\text{Out}}(r,\omega,\ell)\ \mathcal{J}^{\vee B}(\omega,\ell,m)\left[\frac{\ell+1}{r^{2}}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}\hat{e}_{r}-\frac{1}{r}\ \vec{\nabla}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}\right]\\
&+\sum_{\ell m}\frac{\omega^{2}}{\ell(2\ell-1)}G_{{}_{B}}^{\text{Out}}(r,\omega,\ell-1)\ \mathcal{J}^{\vee B}(\omega,\ell,m)\vec{\nabla}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}\\
&-i\omega\ \sum_{\ell m}G_{{}_{E}}^{\text{Out}}(r,\omega,\ell)\ \mathcal{J}^{E}(\omega,\ell,m)\ \vec{\mathbb{U}}_{\ell m}\ .\end{split}

(358)

In
d=3
, the electric outgoing fields are the same as the magnetic ones, i.e.,

\displaystyle\begin{split}G_{{}_{E}}^{\text{Out}}(r,\omega,\ell)=G_{{}_{B}}^{\text{Out}}(r,\omega,\ell)\equiv r^{-\ell}{}_{0}F_{1}\left[\frac{1}{2}-\ell,-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]=\frac{\theta_{\ell}(-i\omega r)}{(2\ell-1)!!}\frac{e^{i\omega r}}{r^{\ell}}\ ,\end{split}

(359)

where the
\theta_{\ell}(z)
are the reverse Bessel polynomials (see Table
4
for explicit expressions). We remind the reader that the above function is essentially the outgoing, spherical Hankel function up to a frequency-dependent normalisation factor:

{}_{0}F_{1}\left[\frac{1}{2}-\ell,-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]=\frac{i(\omega r)^{\ell}}{(2\ell-1)!!}h^{(1)}_{\ell}(\omega r)\ .

(360)

The above expressions for outgoing EM waves are consistent with EM duality, which maps

(\vec{E},\mathcal{J}^{E})\mapsto(\vec{B},\mathcal{J}^{\vee B})\ ,\quad(\vec{B},\mathcal{J}^{\vee B})\mapsto(-\vec{E},-\mathcal{J}^{E})\ .

(361)

The explicit expressions for the spherical multipole moments are given by

\begin{split}\mathcal{J}^{E}(\omega,\ell,\vec{m})&\equiv\frac{1}{(2\ell+1)(\ell+1)}\int_{\vec{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\ r^{\ell-1}\mathscr{Y}^{\ast}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[\ell+\frac{3}{2},-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]\\
&\qquad\times\Bigl\{i\omega r^{2}J^{r}(\vec{r},\omega)-\partial_{r}[r^{2}J^{t}(\vec{r},\omega)]\Bigr\}\ ,\\
\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{J}}$}}^{\vee B}(\omega,\ell,m)&\equiv\frac{1}{2\ell+1}\frac{\ell}{\ell+1}\int_{\vec{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}r^{\ell+1}\mathbb{U}_{I}^{\ell m\ast}(\hat{r}){}_{0}F_{1}\left[\ell+\frac{3}{2},-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]J^{I}(\vec{r},\omega)\\
&=\frac{1}{(2\ell+1)(\ell+1)}\int_{\vec{r}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\ r^{\ell}{}_{0}F_{1}\left[\ell+\frac{3}{2},-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right][\vec{r}\times\vec{J}(\vec{r},\omega)]\cdot\vec{\nabla}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\ .\end{split}

(362)

Here, we have specialised the electric moment of Eq.(
295
) to
d=3
, and we have generalised the magnetic moment in Eq.(
342
) to dynamical situations. The
{}_{0}F_{1}
is the time-delay smearing function and is essentially the spherical Bessel function up to a normalisation:

{}_{0}F_{1}\left[\ell+\frac{3}{2},-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]=\frac{(2\ell+1)!!}{(\omega r)^{\ell}}j_{\ell}(\omega r)=\frac{(2\ell+1)!!}{2^{\ell}\ell!}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-z^{2})^{\ell}\ e^{\pm i\omega zr}\ .

(363)

At large
r
, far away from sources, the EM fields given in Eq.(
358
) become

\begin{split}\vec{E}_{\text{Rad}}(\vec{r},\omega)&=-e^{i\omega r}\sum_{\ell m}\frac{(-i\omega)^{\ell+1}}{\ell(2\ell-1)!!}\left[\mathcal{J}^{E}(\omega,\ell,m)\ \vec{\nabla}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}-\mathcal{J}^{\vee B}(\omega,\ell,m)\ \hat{e}_{r}\times\vec{\nabla}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}\right]\ ,\\
\vec{B}_{\text{Rad}}(\vec{r},\omega)&=-e^{i\omega r}\sum_{\ell m}\frac{(-i\omega)^{\ell+1}}{\ell(2\ell-1)!!}\left[\mathcal{J}^{E}(\omega,\ell,m)\ \hat{e}_{r}\times\vec{\nabla}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}\ +\mathcal{J}^{\vee B}(\omega,\ell,m)\ \vec{\nabla}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}\right]\ .\end{split}

(364)

Here the sum runs over
\ell\geq 1
, and we recognise in these formulae the transverse, radially outgoing EM waves with
\vec{B}_{\text{Rad}}=\hat{e}_{r}\times\vec{E}_{\text{Rad}}
.
The cartesian forms of these equations can be obtained by using the definitions

\begin{split}\sum_{m}\mathcal{J}^{E}(\omega,\ell,m)\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\equiv\frac{(2\ell-1)!!}{\ell!}\ {}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\frac{x^{i_{1}}\ldots x^{i_{\ell}}}{4\pi r^{\ell}}\ ,\\
\sum_{m}\mathcal{J}^{\vee B}(\omega,\ell,m)\mathscr{Y}_{\ell m}(\hat{r})\equiv\frac{(2\ell-1)!!}{\ell!}\ {}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}(\omega)_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\frac{x^{i_{1}}\ldots x^{i_{\ell}}}{4\pi r^{\ell}}\ .\end{split}

(365)

These generalise the relation between spherical and cartesian STF moments beyond statics. The explicit expressions for the cartesian moments are given by

\begin{split}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}&\equiv\frac{1}{\ell+1}\int_{\vec{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\ x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\ldots x^{i_{\ell}>}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[\ell+\frac{3}{2},-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\Bigl\{i\omega rJ^{r}(\vec{r},\omega)-\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}[r^{2}J^{t}(\vec{r},\omega)]\Bigr\}\ ,\\
{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}(\omega)_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}&=\frac{\ell}{\ell+1}\int_{\vec{r}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\ {}_{0}F_{1}\left[\ell+\frac{3}{2},-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right][\vec{r}\times\vec{J}(\vec{r},\omega)]^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\ldots x^{i_{\ell}>}\ .\end{split}

(366)

It is then straightforward to see that Eq.(
351
) and Eq.(
354
) also generalise to time-dependent situations.

The cartesian components of the EM fields can be worked out by directly substituting the above definitions into Eq.(
358
). Alternately, we can take the cartesian Electric fields in general dimensions (i.e. the sum of electric fields in Eq.(
280
) and Eq.(
280
)), and specialise to
d=3
. In either case, once the electric field has been figured out, the magnetic field expressions follow from EM duality. The EM fields outside the sources evaluate to

\begin{split}E_{k}^{\text{Out}}(\vec{r},\omega)&=e^{i\omega r}\sum_{\ell}\frac{\theta_{\ell}(-i\omega r)}{\ell!}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}[(2\ell+1)n^{k}n^{i_{\ell}}-\ell\delta^{ki_{\ell}}]\frac{n^{i_{1}}\ldots n^{i_{\ell-1}}}{4\pi r^{\ell+2}}\\
&\qquad+\omega^{2}e^{i\omega r}\sum_{\ell}\frac{\theta_{\ell-1}(-i\omega r)}{\ell!}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}[\delta^{ki_{\ell}}-n^{k}n^{i_{\ell}}]\frac{n^{i_{1}}\ldots n^{i_{\ell-1}}}{4\pi r^{\ell}}\\
&\qquad+i\omega e^{i\omega r}\varepsilon_{kji_{1}}\sum_{\ell}\frac{\theta_{\ell}(-i\omega r)}{\ell!}{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}(\omega)_{<ji_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\ \frac{n^{i_{1}}\ldots n^{i_{\ell}}}{4\pi r^{\ell+1}},\\
B_{k}^{\text{Out}}(\vec{r},\omega)&=e^{i\omega r}\sum_{\ell}\frac{\theta_{\ell}(-i\omega r)}{\ell!}{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}(\omega)_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}[(2\ell+1)n^{k}n^{i_{\ell}}-\ell\delta^{ki_{\ell}}]\frac{n^{i_{1}}\ldots n^{i_{\ell-1}}}{4\pi r^{\ell+2}}\\
&\qquad+\omega^{2}e^{i\omega r}\sum_{\ell}\frac{\theta_{\ell-1}(-i\omega r)}{\ell!}{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}(\omega)_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}[\delta^{ki_{\ell}}-n^{k}n^{i_{\ell}}]\frac{n^{i_{1}}\ldots n^{i_{\ell-1}}}{4\pi r^{\ell}}\\
&\qquad-i\omega e^{i\omega r}\varepsilon_{kji_{1}}\sum_{\ell}\frac{\theta_{\ell}(-i\omega r)}{\ell!}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<ji_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\ \frac{n^{i_{1}}\ldots n^{i_{\ell}}}{4\pi r^{\ell+1}}\ .\end{split}

(367)

Here we have used a shorthand
n^{i}\equiv\frac{x^{i}}{r}
. The reader might recognise the form appearing in the first line of the fields from the EM fields outside static multipoles. Only the second and the third lines in each field contribute to the radiative part. These are again manifestly duality invariant if we also take

{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}\mapsto{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\ ,\quad{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\mapsto-{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}\ .

(368)

With some relabelling of indices, the radiative EM fields can be cast into the following form

\begin{split}E_{k}^{\text{Rad}}(\vec{r},\omega)&=-\frac{e^{i\omega r}}{4\pi r}\sum_{\ell}\frac{(-i\omega)^{\ell+1}}{\ell!}\\
&\quad\times\Biggl\{{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}[\delta^{ki_{1}}-n^{k}n^{i_{1}}]+\varepsilon_{kji_{1}}{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}(\omega)_{<ji_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\ n^{i_{1}}\Biggr\}n^{i_{2}}\ldots n^{i_{\ell}}\\
B_{k}^{\text{Rad}}(\vec{r},\omega)&=-\frac{e^{i\omega r}}{4\pi r}\sum_{\ell}\frac{(-i\omega)^{\ell+1}}{\ell!}\\
&\quad\times\Biggl\{{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}(\omega)_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}[\delta^{ki_{1}}-n^{k}n^{i_{1}}]-\varepsilon_{kji_{1}}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<ji_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\ n^{i_{1}}\Biggr\}n^{i_{2}}\ldots n^{i_{\ell}}.\end{split}

(369)

The scalar and vector potentials corresponding to the above EM fields can be written down by choosing a gauge. Combining Eqs.(
218
) and (
283
), we can write

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{t}^{\text{Out,New}}(\vec{r},\omega)&=-\sum_{\ell}\frac{G_{{}_{E}}^{\text{Out}}(r,\omega,\ell)}{\ell!\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell-1}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\frac{x^{i_{1}}\ldots x^{i_{\ell}}}{r^{\ell+d-2}}\ ,\\
\mathcal{V}_{k}^{\text{Out,New}}(\vec{r},\omega)&=-i\omega\sum_{\ell>0}\frac{G_{{}_{E}}^{\text{Out}}(r,\omega,\ell-1)}{\ell!\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell-2}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}\ {}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<ki_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell-1}>}\frac{x^{i_{1}}\ldots x^{i_{\ell-1}}}{r^{\ell+d-3}}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{\ell>0}\frac{G_{{}_{B}}^{\text{Out}}(r,\omega,\ell)}{\ell!\mathcal{N}_{d,\ell-1}|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{k<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\frac{x^{i_{1}}\ldots x^{i_{\ell}}}{4\pi r^{\ell+1}}\ .\end{split}

(370)

Specialising to
d=3
and rewriting the magnetic moment part accordingly, we obtain

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{t}^{\text{Out,New}}(\vec{r},\omega)&=-e^{i\omega r}\sum_{\ell}\frac{\theta_{\ell}(-i\omega r)}{\ell!}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\frac{n^{i_{1}}\ldots n^{i_{\ell}}}{4\pi r^{\ell+1}}\ ,\\
\mathcal{V}_{k}^{\text{Out,New}}(\vec{r},\omega)&=-i\omega e^{i\omega r}\sum_{\ell>0}\frac{\theta_{\ell-1}(-i\omega r)}{\ell!}\ {}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<ki_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell-1}>}\frac{n^{i_{1}}\ldots n^{i_{\ell-1}}}{4\pi r^{\ell}}\\
&\qquad+e^{i\omega r}\varepsilon_{kji_{1}}\sum_{\ell>0}\frac{\theta_{\ell}(-i\omega r)}{\ell!}{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}(\omega)_{<ji_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\frac{n^{i_{1}}\ldots n^{i_{\ell}}}{r^{\ell+1}}\ .\end{split}

(371)

These outgoing solutions and their time-reversed counterparts are useful in quantising electrodynamics in spherical coordinates. Far away from the sources, we get the radiative fields

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{t}^{\text{Rad,New}}(\vec{r},\omega)&=-\frac{e^{i\omega r}}{4\pi r}\sum_{\ell}\frac{(-i\omega)^{\ell}}{\ell!}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}n^{i_{1}}\ldots n^{i_{\ell}}\ ,\\
\mathcal{V}_{k}^{\text{Rad,New}}(\vec{r},\omega)&=\frac{e^{i\omega r}}{4\pi r}\sum_{\ell>0}\frac{(-i\omega)^{\ell}}{\ell!}\Biggl\{{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<ki_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell-1}>}+\varepsilon_{kji_{\ell}}n_{i_{\ell}}{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}(\omega)_{<ji_{1}\ldots i_{\ell-1}>}\Biggr\}n^{i_{1}}\ldots n^{i_{\ell-1}}\ .\end{split}

(372)

Power loss in
d=3

We will now show how the power loss formulae in general dimension can be cast into familiar forms when
d=3
. The main point is to rewrite the magnetic multipole power loss to make the EM duality manifest. In terms of spherical multipoles, this is easy: we start with the power radiated in terms of our multipole moments is

\mathcal{P}(\omega)=\sum_{\ell\vec{m}}\frac{2\pi}{\Gamma(\nu)^{2}}\frac{\omega^{2\nu+1}}{2^{2\nu}}\frac{\ell+d-2}{\ell}|\mathcal{J}^{E}(\omega,\ell,\vec{m})|^{2}+\sum_{\alpha\ell\vec{m}}\frac{2\pi}{\Gamma(\nu)^{2}}\frac{\omega^{2\nu+1}}{2^{2\nu}}|\mathcal{J}^{B}(\omega,\alpha,\ell,\vec{m})|^{2},

(373)

and rewrite it in
d=3
using Eq.(
342
) to get

\mathcal{P}(\omega)_{d=3}=\sum_{\ell m}\frac{\omega^{2\ell+2}}{[(2\ell-1)!!]^{2}}\frac{\ell+1}{\ell}\left[|\mathcal{J}^{E}(\omega,\ell,m)|^{2}+|\mathcal{J}^{\vee B}(\omega,\ell,m)|^{2}\right]\ .

(374)

This expression is now manifestly invariant under EM duality.

Power loss in terms of STF moments requires a bit more work: the
d=3
version of Eq.(
314
) is

\begin{split}\mathcal{P}(\omega)_{d=3}&=\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{\ell+1}{\ell}\frac{\omega^{2\ell+2}}{(2\ell+1)!!}\ \frac{1}{\ell!}\ \left[{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]^{\ast}\ \left[{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{\omega^{2\ell+2}}{(2\ell+1)!!}\ \frac{1}{\ell!}\ \left[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{k<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]^{\ast}\ \left[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}(\omega)_{k<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]\\
\end{split}

(375)

We want to rewrite this formula in terms of
{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
tensor. Using Eq.(
351
), we can write

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\ell!}[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}_{k<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}]^{\ast}[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{k<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}]|_{d=3}\\
&=\frac{1}{\ell!}\left[\frac{1}{\ell}\sum_{p=1}^{\ell}\varepsilon_{kqi_{p}}{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee<qi_{1}i_{2}\dots i_{\underline{p}}\ldots i_{\ell-1}>}\right]^{\ast}\left[\frac{1}{\ell}\sum_{p^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}\varepsilon_{kq^{\prime}i_{p^{\prime}}}{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}_{<q^{\prime}i_{1}i_{2}\dots i_{\underline{p^{\prime}}}\ldots i_{\ell-1}>}\right]\ .\end{split}

(376)

In this product of sums made of
\ell^{2}
terms, we get
\ell
terms with
p=p^{\prime}
and
\ell(\ell-1)
terms with
p\neq p^{\prime}
. Here every
p\neq p^{\prime}
term evaluates to

\begin{split}\varepsilon_{kq_{1}i_{1}}&\varepsilon_{kq_{2}i_{2}}[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee<q_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\dots i_{\ell}>}]^{\ast}[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}_{<q_{2}i_{1}i_{3}\dots i_{\ell}>}]\\
&=(\delta_{q_{1}q_{2}}\delta_{i_{1}i_{2}}-\delta_{q_{1}i_{2}}\delta_{q_{2}i_{1}})[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee<q_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\dots i_{\ell}>}]^{\ast}[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}_{<q_{2}i_{1}i_{3}\dots i_{\ell}>}]\\
&=[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee<i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\dots i_{\ell}>}][{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}_{<i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\dots i_{\ell}>}]\ .\end{split}

(377)

Here, the second term evaluates to zero because of the trace-free condition on
{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
.
Every
p=p^{\prime}
term evaluates to

\begin{split}\varepsilon_{kq_{1}i_{1}}\varepsilon_{kq_{2}i_{1}}[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee<q_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\dots i_{\ell}>}]^{\ast}[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}_{<q_{2}i_{2}i_{3}\dots i_{\ell}>}]&=2\ [{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee<i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\dots i_{\ell}>}]^{\ast}[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}_{<i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\dots i_{\ell}>}]\ .\end{split}

(378)

Adding up all the terms yields then a factor of
\ell(\ell-1)+2\ell=\ell(\ell+1)
. Thus, we have proved that

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\ell!}[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}_{k<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}]^{\ast}[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{k<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}]|_{d=3}&=\frac{\ell+1}{\ell}\times\frac{1}{\ell!}[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee<i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\dots i_{\ell}>}]^{\ast}[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}_{<i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\dots i_{\ell}>}]\ .\end{split}

(379)

This identity can be used to recast the power loss formula in Eq.(
375
) into a duality invariant form:

\begin{split}\mathcal{P}(\omega)_{d=3}&=\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{\ell+1}{\ell}\frac{\omega^{2\ell+2}}{(2\ell+1)!!}\\
&\quad\times\frac{1}{\ell!}\ \Biggl\{\left[{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]^{\ast}\ \left[{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}_{<i_{1}\ldots i_{\ell}>}\right]+[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee<i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\dots i_{\ell}>}]^{\ast}[{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}_{<i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}\dots i_{\ell}>}]\Biggr\}\ .\end{split}

(380)

The first few terms here correspond to electric/magnetic dipole/quadrupole moments, i.e., if we set

\begin{split}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}_{i_{1}}&\equiv d^{E}_{i_{1}}\ ,\quad{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}_{<i_{1}i_{2}>}\equiv q^{E}_{<i_{1}i_{2}>}\ ,\quad{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}_{ki_{1}}\equiv d^{B}_{i_{1}}\ ,\quad{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}_{<i_{1}i_{2}>}=q^{B}_{<i_{1}i_{2}>}\ .\end{split}

(381)

The power loss formula then takes the form

\begin{split}\mathcal{P}(\omega)_{d=3}&=\frac{\omega^{4}}{6\pi}\sum_{i}|d^{E}_{i}|^{2}+\frac{\omega^{6}}{80\pi}\sum_{ij}|q^{E}_{<ij>}|^{2}+\ldots\\
&\quad+\frac{\omega^{4}}{6\pi}\sum_{i}|d^{B}_{i}|^{2}+\frac{\omega^{6}}{80\pi}\sum_{ij}|q^{B}_{<ij>}|^{2}+\ldots\end{split}

(382)

This agrees with the Larmor formula quoted in standard textbooks.
32
32

32

For example, Jackson defines his cartesian STF electric quadrupole moment to be three times our quadrupole moment, and his definition of power loss in the frequency domain is half of ours due to Fourier transform conventions. So, in his textbook, he gives
2\times 6\pi=12\pi
as the denominator for dipole power loss and
2\times 3^{2}\times 80\pi=1440\pi
as the denominator for quadrupole power loss.

C.4
Comparison of normalisations

In our discussion of multipole expansions, we have chosen our multipole moment definitions uniformly across statics and radiation, and we have tried to use the simplest normalisations consistent with electric-magnetic duality. This is consistent with modern treatments of gravitational multipole expansions based on STF tensors. Unfortunately, our notations differ from popular textbooks on electromagnetism where
radiative
multipole moments are treated very differently from
static
multipole moments. This fact complicates the comparison of our expression with those available in standard EM textbooks like that of Jackson
[
45
]
and Zangwill
[
46
]
. Our normalisations also differ slightly from papers on STF multipole expansion
[
67
,
87
,
69
]
. Given this bewildering array of existent normalisations for EM multipoles, we will conclude this appendix by providing the necessary dictionary for translation to the notations in such texts and papers. There are no new results here, and a reader disinterested in notational fine print may safely skip what follows.

Our expressions can be converted to static moments appearing in textbooks via

\begin{split}\bar{\mathcal{J}}^{E}(\ell,m)&=\frac{1}{2\ell+1}q^{\text{Jackson}}_{\ell m}=\frac{1}{4\pi}A^{\text{Zangwill}}_{\ell m}=\frac{Q^{(e)\text{LBP}}_{\ell m}}{\sqrt{4\pi(2\ell+1)}}\ ,\\
\overline{\raisebox{0.0pt}[1.1pt]{${\mathcal{J}}$}}^{\vee B}(\ell,m)&=\ell\frac{\mu^{\text{Jackson}}_{\ell m}}{\sqrt{4\pi(2\ell+1)}}=\frac{1}{2\ell+1}M^{\text{Zangwill}}_{\ell m}=\frac{Q^{(m)\text{LBP}}_{\ell m}}{\sqrt{4\pi(2\ell+1)}}\ .\end{split}

(383)

These factors can be figured out by comparing our static moments (i.e., Eqs.(
241
) and (
342
)) against the definitions given in these texts.
33
33

33

The static moments of Jackson
[
45
]
are defined in JEq(4.3) and Problem(5.8b), that of Zangwill
[
46
]
are defined in ZEq.(4.87) and ZEq.(11.66), and of Lifshitz-Berestetskii-Pitaevskii
[
98
]
are given in LBPEq.(46.7) and LBPEq(47.3).
As indicated in the footnote, Jackson does not define a magnetostatic multipole moment in his text. Instead, in Problem(5.8b) involving axisymmetric currents of the form
\vec{J}=J_{\varphi}(r,\theta)\hat{\varphi}
, Jackson defines

\mu^{\text{Jackson}}_{\ell,m=0}=-\frac{1}{\ell(\ell+1)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}r^{\ell}P^{1}_{\ell}(\cos\vartheta)\bar{J}_{\varphi}(r,\theta)=\frac{1}{\ell+1}\sqrt{\frac{4\pi}{2\ell+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}r^{\ell+1}\vec{\mathbb{U}}^{\ast}_{\ell,m=0}(\hat{r})\cdot\bar{J}(\vec{r})\ ,

(384)

where we have used the fact that

\vec{r}\times\vec{\nabla}\mathscr{Y}_{\ell,m=0}=\hat{e}_{\varphi}\sqrt{\frac{2\ell+1}{4\pi}}P^{1}_{\ell}(\cos\vartheta)\ .

(385)

We have generalised Jackson’s definition to general
m
to determine the relative normalisation quoted above. With this dictionary, we have checked that the multipole expansions for static fields given in these texts agree with our expressions.

Moving on to radiative multipole moments, the relative normalisations are given by
34
34

34

This follows from comparing Zangwill’s ZEq.(20.224),(20.225), as well as Jackson’s JEq.( 9.167), (9.168) against our Eq.(
362
).

\begin{split}\Lambda_{\ell m}^{E,\text{Zangwill}}(\omega)&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell(\ell+1)}}\ a_{E}^{\text{Jackson}}(\omega,\ell,m)=-i\frac{\omega^{\ell+2}}{\ell(2\ell-1)!!}\ \mathcal{J}^{E}(\omega,\ell,m)\ ,\\
\Lambda_{\ell m}^{M,\text{Zangwill}}(\omega)&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ell(\ell+1)}}a_{M}^{\text{Jackson}}(\omega,\ell,m)=i\frac{\omega^{\ell+2}}{\ell(2\ell-1)!!}\mathcal{J}^{\vee B}(\omega,\ell,m)\ .\end{split}

(386)

Our normalisations are closer to that of Campbell-Macek-Morgan
[
84
]
with

\begin{split}Q^{\text{CMM}}_{\ell m}(\omega)=(2\ell+1)\mathcal{J}^{E}(\omega,\ell,m)\ ,\qquad M^{\text{CMM}}_{\ell m}(\omega)=(2\ell+1)\mathcal{J}^{\vee B}(\omega,\ell,m)\ .\end{split}

(387)

With this dictionary, we have checked that the multipole expansions for radiative fields as well as power loss given in these texts agree with our expressions.
35
35

35

Note that power loss formulae in Jackson’s chapter§9 are half of ours due to differences in Fourier transform conventions.

We now turn to STF multipole moments. In general
d
, the authors Amalberti-Larrouturou-Yang (ALY)
[
69
]
define STF electric and magnetic moments. Their definitions are related to ours by

\begin{split}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}&=I_{<i_{1}i_{2}\dots i_{\ell}>}^{\text{ALY}}\ ,\quad{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}_{i<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}=\frac{2\ell}{\ell+1}J_{i<i_{1}i_{2}\dots i_{\ell}>}^{\text{ALY}}\ .\end{split}

(388)

These normalisations can be fixed by comparing the Fourier transform of ALYEq(3.17) against our definitions in Eq.(
298
) and Eq.(
214
). For the vector projector, ALY seem to use Eq.(
142
), but their formula seems to omit the final symmetrisation. The conversion rule quoted above assumes that such a symmetrisation is implicit in their expressions.
36
36

36

The reader should note that ALY’s anti-symmetric projection involves an additional factor of half compared to our conventions here.

The rest of the references dealing with STF moments are specific to
d=3
: Damour-Iyer
[
67
]
and Ross
[
87
]
. We claim that the relative normalisation factors are

\begin{split}{}^{E}\mathcal{Q}_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}&=Q_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}^{\text{Damour-Iyer}}=I^{<i_{1}i_{2}\dots i_{\ell}>}_{\text{Ross}}\ ,\\
{}^{B}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}&=\frac{\ell}{\ell+1}M_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}^{\text{Damour-Iyer}}=\frac{\ell}{\ell+1}J^{<i_{1}i_{2}\dots i_{\ell}>}_{\text{Ross}}\ .\end{split}

(389)

Here, we have converted the time-domain expressions of
[
67
,
87
]
into the frequency domain. The Damour-Iyer definitions in DIeq(4.18) of
[
67
]
can be Fourier transformed to frequency domain as

\begin{split}Q^{\text{Damour-Iyer}}_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}&=\frac{1}{\ell+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\ \ x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\ldots x^{i_{\ell}>}\int\limits_{-1}^{1}dz\ \frac{(2\ell+1)!!}{2^{\ell+1}\ell!}(1-z^{2})^{\ell}e^{-i\omega rz}\\
&\qquad\times\left[(\ell+1-i\omega rz)J^{t}(\vec{r},\omega)+i\omega J^{r}(\vec{r},\omega)\right]\ ,\\
M^{\text{Damour-Iyer}}_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}&=\frac{1}{\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\ \int\limits_{-1}^{1}dz\ \frac{(2\ell+1)!!}{2^{\ell+1}\ell!}(1-z^{2})^{\ell}e^{-i\omega rz}\ x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\ldots x^{i_{\ell}>}(\vec{r}\times\vec{\nabla})\cdot\vec{J}(\vec{r},\omega)\ .\end{split}

(390)

To relate it to Eq.(
366
), we perform an integration by parts:

\begin{split}Q^{\text{Damour-Iyer}}_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}&=\frac{1}{\ell+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\ \ x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\ldots x^{i_{\ell}>}\int\limits_{-1}^{1}dz\ \frac{(2\ell+1)!!}{2^{\ell+1}\ell!}(1-z^{2})^{\ell}e^{-i\omega rz}\\
&\qquad\times\Bigl\{i\omega rJ^{r}(\vec{r},\omega)-\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}[r^{2}J^{t}(\vec{r},\omega)]\Bigr\}\ ,\\
M^{\text{Damour-Iyer}}_{<i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{\ell}>}&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\ \int\limits_{-1}^{1}dz\ \frac{(2\ell+1)!!}{2^{\ell+1}\ell!}(1-z^{2})^{\ell}e^{-i\omega rz}\ [\vec{r}\times\vec{J}(\vec{r},\omega)]^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\ldots x^{i_{\ell}>}\ .\end{split}

(391)

Invoking Eq.(
363
), we then obtain the normalisations claimed above.

As for Ross
[
87
]
, his magnetic moment expressions are directly of the form Eq.(
363
). After converting to the frequency domain, his electric moment definition is

\begin{split}&I(\omega)^{<i_{1}i_{2}\dots i_{\ell}>}_{\text{Ross}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\ \Bigg\{{}_{1}F_{2}\left[\frac{\ell}{2}\ ;\frac{\ell}{2}+1\ ,\ell+\frac{3}{2}\ ;-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]J^{t}(\vec{r},\omega)\ x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\dots x^{i_{\ell}>}\\
&-i\omega\frac{\ell}{(\ell+1)(\ell+2)}{}_{1}F_{2}\left[\frac{\ell}{2}+1\ ;\frac{\ell}{2}+2\ ,\ell+\frac{3}{2}\ ;-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]\\
&\qquad\times\left\{r^{2}J^{<i_{\ell}}(\vec{r},\omega)\ x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\dots x^{i_{\ell-1}>}-x_{k}J^{k}(\vec{r},\omega)\ x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\dots x^{i_{\ell}>}\right\}\Bigg\}\ .\end{split}

(392)

From charge conservation, we have the following identity:

\begin{split}&-i\omega\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\ J^{0}(\vec{r},\omega)\ r^{2p}x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\dots x^{i_{\ell}>}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\ \partial_{k}J^{k}(\vec{r},\omega)\ r^{2p}x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\dots x^{i_{\ell}>}\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}r^{2p-2}\left[\ell\ J^{<i_{\ell}}(\vec{r},\omega)\ x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\dots x^{i_{\ell-1}>}+2p\ x_{k}J^{k}(\vec{r},\omega)\ x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\dots x^{i_{\ell}>}\right]\end{split}

(393)

We use this to write

\begin{split}&I(\omega)^{<i_{1}i_{2}\dots i_{\ell}>}_{\text{Ross}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\ \Bigg\{{}_{1}F_{2}\left[\frac{\ell}{2}\ ;\frac{\ell}{2}+1\ ,\ell+\frac{3}{2}\ ;-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]J^{t}(\vec{r},\omega)\ x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\dots x^{i_{\ell}>}\\
&-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{(\ell+1)(\ell+2)}{}_{1}F_{2}\left[\frac{\ell}{2}+1\ ;\frac{\ell}{2}+2\ ,\ell+\frac{3}{2}\ ;-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]J^{t}(\vec{r},\omega)\ x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\dots x^{i_{\ell}>}\\
&-\frac{1}{(\ell+1)}\Big\{{}_{1}F_{2}\left[\frac{\ell}{2}+1\ ;\frac{\ell}{2}+2\ ,\ell+\frac{3}{2}\ ;-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]\\
&\qquad-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{(\ell+4)(2\ell+3)}{}_{1}F_{2}\left[\frac{\ell}{2}+2\ ;\frac{\ell}{2}+3\ ,\ell+\frac{5}{2}\ ;-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]\Big\}x_{k}J^{k}(\vec{r},\omega)\ x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\dots x^{i_{\ell}>}\Bigg\}\ .\end{split}

(394)

The above expression can then be further simplified to

\begin{split}I(\omega)^{<i_{1}i_{2}\dots i_{\ell}>}_{\text{Ross}}=&\frac{1}{\ell+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\ \Bigg\{\frac{1}{r^{\ell}}\partial_{r}\left(r^{\ell+1}{}_{0}F_{1}\left[\ell+\frac{3}{2},-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]\right)J^{t}(\vec{r},\omega)\ x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\dots x^{i_{\ell}>}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+i\omega{}_{0}F_{1}\left[\ell+\frac{3}{2},-\frac{\omega^{2}r^{2}}{4}\right]x_{k}J^{k}(\vec{r},\omega)\ x^{<i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}\dots x^{i_{\ell}>}\Bigg\}\ .\end{split}

(395)

After an integration by parts, this matches our definition. We have also checked that the power loss formula and the radiative fields given by Ross agrees with our expressions.

Dimensional analysis

This section provides a list of scaling dimensions of various physical quantities defined in our analysis for the reader’s convenience. This allows for easy checks on the dimensional compatibility of all the equations. We work in units where the speed of light(
c
), the permittivity of vacuum(
\epsilon_{0}
) and the reduced Planck’s constant(
\hbar
) are set to one. This leaves us a single scaling dimension, which we pick to be mass
[M]
.

Mass dimensions of various quantities in the frequency domain. The electric quantities appear in the second and the third column, whereas the magnetic counterparts appear in the third and the fourth.
